7.9  Feature / Ebedding Learning
The following uses embeddings to make predictions.  The running example is
the (user, item, rating) MovieLens subset. Instead of using the genres of movies
as the features, it learns movie features.  The features and weights are embed-
dings.  The prediction for a user on a movie is the dot product of the movie
embedding and user embedding, as well as bias terms.  It is instructive to see
how important the bias is.
An Embedding consists of a vector and a bias term. When a new data point
arrives, as part of a batch, absorb_error is used to determine the deltas, which
are then cobined to update the weights at the end of a batch. This implements
stochastic gradient descent on the weights (the bias and the vector values).
learnEmbedding.py — Learning embeddings
11  import random
12  import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
13  import csv
14  from learnProblem import Learner
15  from learnRatings import Ratings, RatingsBool, RatingsNum, RatingsCat
16  from learnLinear import sigmoid
17  from display import Displayable
18
19  class Embedding(Displayable):
20      def __init__(self, learner):
21         """An embedding for an entity.
22         The learner provides parameters needed for the update.
23         """
24         self.learner = learner
25         self.bias = 0
26         self.vector =
[random.uniform(-learner.property_range,learner.property_range)
27                             for p in range(learner.emb_size)]
28         self.bias_delta = 0
29         self.vector_delta = [0 for p in range(learner.emb_size)]
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

2047.  Supervised Machine Learning
30
31      def absorb_error(self, error, other_emb):
32         """absorb error
33         error is the error
34         other is the embedding of the other entity
35         """
36         self.bias_delta += error
37         for p in range(self.learner.emb_size):
38             self.vector_delta[p] += error*other_emb[p]
39
40      def update(self):
41         self.bias -= self.learner.step_size*self.bias_delta
42         self.bias -= self.learner.reglz*self.bias # regularize
43         self.bias_delta = 0
44         for p in range(self.learner.emb_size):
45             self.vector[p] -= self.learner.step_size*self.vector_delta[p]
46             self.vector[p] -= self.learner.reglz*self.vector[p] # regularize
47             self.vector_delta[p] = 0
learnEmbedding.py — (continued)
49  class Emb_learner(Learner):
50      """Entity Embedding (Matrix factorization) Learner"""
51      def __init__(self,
52                 dataset,          # a dataset
53                 step_size = 0.01,    # gradient descent step size
54                 regularization = 1.0, # L2 regularization for full dataset
55                 emb_size = 10, # number of hidden properties
56                 property_range = 0.02 # properties are initialized to be
between
57                                      # -property_range and property_range
58                 ):
59         self.dataset = dataset
60         self.training_data = dataset.train
61         self.test_data = self.dataset.test
62         self.step_size = step_size
63         self.regularization = regularization
64         self.emb_size = emb_size
65         self.property_range = property_range
66         self.num_ratings = len(self.training_data)
67         self.average = (sum(self.dataset.target(e) for e in
self.training_data)
68                          /self.num_ratings)
69         self.users = {u for (u,_,_) in self.training_data}
70         self.items = {i for (_,i,_) in self.training_data}
71         self.user_embs = {u: Embedding(self) for u in self.users}
72         self.item_embs = {i: Embedding(self) for i in self.items}
73         self.epoch = 0
74         self.display(1, f"Predict mean ({self.average:.3f}) (Ave
Abs,AveSumSq) "
75                    f"training={self.eval2string(self.training_data,
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

7.9.  Feature / Ebedding Learning205
useMean=True)} "
76                    f"test={self.eval2string(self.test_data, useMean=True)}")
77
78      def prediction(self,user,item):
79         """Returns prediction for this user on this item.
80         user or item could be in test set but not in the training set
81         """
82         if user in self.users: # user in training set
83             if item in self.items: # item in training set
84                res = (self.average
85                       + self.user_embs[user].bias
86                       + self.item_embs[item].bias
87                       +
sum([self.user_embs[user].vector[p]*self.item_embs[item].vector[p]
88                            for p in range(self.emb_size)]))
89             else: # training set contains user but not item
90                res = self.average + self.user_embs[user].bias
91         elif item in self.items: # training set contains item but not user
92             res = self.average + self.item_embs[item].bias
93         else:
94             res = self.average
95         if self.dataset.target.ftype == "boolean":
96             return sigmoid(res)
97         else:
98             return res
99
100      def learn(self, num_epochs = 20, batch_size=1000):
101         """ do (approximately) num_epochs iterations through the dataset
102         batch_size is the size of each batch of stochastic gradient
gradient descent.
103         """
104         batch_size = min(int(batch_size), len(self.training_data))
105         batchs_per_epoch = len(self.training_data) // batch_size #
approximate
106         num_iter = num_epochs*batchs_per_epoch
107         self.reglz =
self.step_size*self.regularization*batch_size/len(self.training_data)
#regularization per batch
108
109         for i in range(num_iter):
110             # determine errors for a batch
111             for (user,item,rating) in random.sample(self.training_data,
batch_size):
112                actual = self.dataset.target((user,item,rating))
113                error = self.prediction(user,item) - actual
114                self.user_embs[user].absorb_error(error,
self.item_embs[item].vector)
115                self.item_embs[item].absorb_error(error,
self.user_embs[user].vector)
116                """
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

2067.  Supervised Machine Learning
117                self.user_embs[user].bias_delta += error
118                self.item_embs[item].bias_delta += error
119                for p in range(self.emb_size):
120                    self.user_embs[user].vector_delta[p] +=
error*self.item_embs[item].vector[p]
121                    self.item_embs[item].vector_delta[p] +=
error*self.user_emb.vector[user][p]
122                """
123             # Update all parameters
124             for ur in self.users:
125                self.user_embs[ur].update()
126             for it in self.items:
127                self.item_embs[it].update()
128             if i % batchs_per_epoch == batchs_per_epoch-1:
129                self.epoch += 1
130                self.display(2,"Epoch", self.epoch, "(Ave Abs,AveSumSq)",
131                           "training =",self.eval2string(self.training_data),
132                           "test =",self.eval2string(self.test_data))
133
134      def evaluate(self, ratings, useMean=False):
135         """returns (average_absolute_error, average_sum_squares_error) for
ratings
136         """
137         abs_error = 0
138         sumsq_error = 0
139         if not ratings: return (0,0)
140         for triple in ratings:
141             (user,item,rating) = triple
142             actual = self.dataset.target(triple)
143             prediction = self.average if useMean else
self.prediction(user,item)
144             error = prediction - actual
145             abs_error += abs(error)
146             sumsq_error += error * error
147         return abs_error/len(ratings), sumsq_error/len(ratings)
148
149      def eval2string(self, *args, **nargs):
150         """returns a string form of evaluate, with fewer digits
151         """
152         (abs,ssq) = self.evaluate(*args, **nargs)
153         return f"({abs:.4f}, {ssq:.4f})"
The plot_predictions method plots the predictions, in order, for each value of
the target. Figure 7.4 shows the result for a Boolean target, with an embedding
size  of  20  with  50  and  500  iterations.   With  more  iterations,  the  training  set
becomes closer to (0,1) and (1,0),  but the test set gets worse.   Note how it is
better at predicting for when the actual is true than when it is false.   For 50
iterations, the test graph shows that it predicted less that 0.5 only 38% of the
time when the actual was false.  It predicted less that 0.5 only 11% of the time
when the actual was true.

Exercise 7.24  For each of these, use the provided test set as the validation set, and
test on a different time period; either the days following (which is likely to include
many of the same users and movies), or a completely different time period (which
is unlikely to include the same users, and may include some of the same movies).
Try to optimize the following parameters on the test/validations set. Use squared
error for real prediction and log loss for Boolen predictions.
(a)  the number of iterations
(b)  the L2 regularizer
(c)  the embedding size.
7.9.1  Understanding Embeddings
The following code can create figures like Figure 7.6 (see also Figure 17.1 of
Poole  and  Mackworth[2023]).   To  understand  this,  think  about  what  ratings
you would expect when the magnitude of the embddings is high,  and both
user and item embeddings are positive, when one is positive and the other is
negative, or they are both negative. What if one embedding is close to zero?
This figure is designed to visualize how emebddings relate to ratings.  It is
difficult to see many many ratings on a plot, and it is important to see how the
ratings change, rather than their absolute value, given that they are added to
biases. For these reasons, we create a much smaller dataset which only includes
the items and people with the most ratings.

215      def __init__(self, rating_set, num_items = (20,30), num_users =
(20,30)):
216         """Returns a subset of the ratings by picking the most rated items,
217         and then the users that have most ratings on these, and then all of
the
218         ratings that involve these users and items.
219         num_items is (ni,si) which selects ni users at random from the top
si users
220         num_users is (nu,su) which selects nu items at random from the top
su items
221         """
222         (ni, si) = num_items
223         (nu, su) = num_users
224         items = {item for (user,item,rating) in rating_set.train}
225         item_counts = {i:0 for i in items}
226         for (user,item,rating) in rating_set.train:
227             item_counts[item] += 1
228
229         items_sorted = sorted((item_counts[i],i) for i in items)
230         if ni<si:
231             top_items = random.sample([item for (count, item) in
items_sorted[-si:]], ni)
232         else:
233             top_items = [item for (count, item) in items_sorted[-si:]]
234         set_top_items = set(top_items)
235         self.display(2,f"{top_items =}")
236
237         users = {user for (user,item,rating) in rating_set.train}
238         user_counts = {u:0 for u in users}
239         for (user,item,rating) in rating_set.train:
240             if item in set_top_items:
241                user_counts[user] += 1
242
243         users_sorted = sorted((user_counts[u],u) for u in users)
244         if nu < su:
245            top_users = random.sample([user for (count, user) in
users_sorted[-su:]], nu)
246         else:
247             top_users = [user for (count, user) in users_sorted[-su:]]
248         set_top_users = set(top_users)
249         self.display(2,f"{top_users =}")
250
251         self.train = [ (user,item,rating)
252                         for (user,item,rating) in rating_set.train
253                         if user in set_top_users and item in set_top_items]
254         self.test = []
255         self.input_features = rating_set.input_features # so it can be used
with features

302  def test_prop(num = (30,40), score_thresh=0.1, seed=None):
303      """Show the property plot.
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

2147.  Supervised Machine Learning
304      num=(10,10) pick top 10 (repeatable and small enough to see)
305      num=(10,30) pick 10 out of 30 (not deterministic)
306      """

learner_s.users),
329                         reverse=True)
330      #  pick users
331      (current_score, user) = user_scores[0]
332      picked_users = [user]
333      print(f"{fval:.3f}\t{user}")
334      for (fval, nuser) in user_scores[1:]:
335         if abs(current_score - fval) >= score_thresh:
336             picked_users.append(nuser)
337             current_score = fval
338             print(f"{fval:.3f}\t{nuser}")
339      plot_property(learner_s, 0, plot_all=True,
340                 users=picked_users,
341                  items=picked_items)
342      learner_s.picked_users = picked_users
343      learner_s.picked_items = picked_items

7.10  Boosting
The following code implements functional gradient boosting for regression.
A  Boosted  dataset  is  created  from  a  base  dataset  by  subtracting  the  pre-
diction of the offset function from each example.  This does not save the new
dataset, but generates it as needed. The extra space used is constant, indepen-
dent on the size of the dataset.
learnBoosting.py — Functional Gradient Boosting
11  from learnProblem import Data_set, Learner, Loss
12  from learnNoInputs import Predict
13  from learnLinear import sigmoid
14  import statistics
15  import random
16
17  class Boosted_dataset(Data_set):
18      def __init__(self, base_dataset, offset_fun, subsample=1.0):
19         """new dataset which is like base_dataset,
20            but offset_fun(e) is subtracted from the target of each example e
21         """
22         self.base_dataset = base_dataset
23         self.offset_fun = offset_fun
24         self.train =
random.sample(base_dataset.train,int(subsample*len(base_dataset.train)))
25         self.valid = base_dataset.valid
26         #Data_set.__init__(self, base_dataset.train, base_dataset.valid,
27         #                base_dataset.prob_valid, base_dataset.target_index)
28
29         #def create_features(self):
30         """creates new features - called at end of Data_set.init()
31         defines a new target
32         """
33         self.input_features = self.base_dataset.input_features
34         def newout(e):
35             return self.base_dataset.target(e) - self.offset_fun(e)
36         newout.frange = self.base_dataset.target.frange
37         newout.ftype = self.infer_type(newout.frange)
38         self.target = newout
39
40      def conditions(self, *args, colsample_bytree=0.5, **nargs):
41         conds = self.base_dataset.conditions(*args, **nargs)
42         return random.sample(conds, int(colsample_bytree*len(conds)))
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

7.10.  Boosting217
A boosting learner takes in a dataset and a base learner, and returns a new
predictor. The base learner, takes a dataset, and returns a Learner object.
learnBoosting.py — (continued)
44  class Boosting_learner(Learner):
45      def __init__(self, dataset, base_learner_class, subsample=0.8):
46         self.dataset = dataset
47         self.base_learner_class = base_learner_class
48         self.subsample = subsample
49         mean = sum(self.dataset.target(e)
50                   for e in self.dataset.train)/len(self.dataset.train)
51         self.predictor = lambda e:mean  # function that returns mean for
each example
52         self.predictor.__doc__ = "lambda e:"+str(mean)
53         self.offsets = [self.predictor] # list of base learners
54         self.predictors = [self.predictor] # list of predictors
55         self.errors = [data.evaluate_dataset(data.valid, self.predictor,
Loss.squared)]
56         self.display(1,"Mean validation set squared loss=", self.errors[0] )
57
58
59      def learn(self, num_ensembles=10):
60         """adds num_ensemble learners to the ensemble.
61         returns a new predictor.
62         """
63         for i in range(num_ensembles):
64             train_subset = Boosted_dataset(self.dataset, self.predictor,
subsample=self.subsample)
65             learner = self.base_learner_class(train_subset)
66             new_offset = learner.learn()
67             self.offsets.append(new_offset)
68             def new_pred(e, old_pred=self.predictor, off=new_offset):
69                return old_pred(e)+off(e)
70             self.predictor = new_pred
71             self.predictors.append(new_pred)
72             self.errors.append(data.evaluate_dataset(data.valid,
self.predictor, Loss.squared))
73             self.display(1,f"Iteration {len(self.offsets)-1},treesize =
{new_offset.num_leaves}. mean squared
loss={self.errors[-1]}")
74         return self.predictor
For testing, sp_DT_learner returns a learner that predicts the mean at the leaves
and is evaluated using squared loss.  It can also take arguments to change the
default arguments for the trees.

7.10.1  Gradient Tree Boosting
The following implements gradient Boosted trees for classification. If you want
to  use  this  gradient  tree  boosting  for  a  real  problem,  we  recommend  using
XGBoost [Chen and Guestrin, 2016] or LightGBM [Ke, Meng, Finley, Wang,
Chen, Ma, Ye, and Liu, 2017].
GTB_learner subclasses DT_learner.   The method learn_tree is used un-
changed. DT_learner assumes that the value at the leaf is the prediction of the
leaf, thus leaf_value needs to be overridden.  It also assumes that all nodes
at a leaf have the same prediction, but in GTB the elements of a leaf can have
different values, depending on the previous trees. Thus sum_losses also needs
to be overridden.
learnBoosting.py — (continued)
124  class GTB_learner(DT_learner):
125      def __init__(self, dataset, number_trees, lambda_reg=1, gamma=0,
**dtargs):
126         DT_learner.__init__(self, dataset, loss=Loss.log, **dtargs)
127         self.number_trees = number_trees
128         self.lambda_reg = lambda_reg
129         self.gamma = gamma
130         self.trees = []
131
132      def learn(self):
133         conds = self.dataset.conditions(self.max_num_cuts)
134         for i in range(self.number_trees):
135             tree = self.learn_tree(conds, self.train)
136             self.trees.append(tree)
137             self.display(1,"Iteration",i, "treesize =", tree.num_leaves,
138                         "train logloss =",
139                         self.dataset.evaluate_dataset(self.dataset.train,
140                                                      self.gtb_predictor,
Loss.log),
141                         "validation logloss =",
142                            self.dataset.evaluate_dataset(self.dataset.valid,
143                                           self.gtb_predictor, Loss.log))
144         return self.gtb_predictor
145
146      def gtb_predictor(self, example, extra=0):
147         """prediction for example,
148         extras is an extra contribution for this example being considered
149         """
150         return sigmoid(sum(t(example) for t in self.trees)+extra)
151
152      def leaf_value(self, egs, domain=[0,1]):
153         """value at the leaves for examples egs
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

2207.  Supervised Machine Learning
154         domain argument is ignored"""
155         pred_acts = [(self.gtb_predictor(e),self.target(e)) for e in egs]
156         return sum(a-p for (p,a) in pred_acts) /(sum(p*(1-p) for (p,a) in
pred_acts)+self.lambda_reg)
157
158
159      def sum_losses(self, data_subset):
160         """returns sum of losses for dataset (assuming a leaf is formed
with no more splits)
161         """
162         leaf_val = self.leaf_value(data_subset)
163         error = sum(Loss.log(self.gtb_predictor(e,leaf_val), self.target(e))
164                     for e in data_subset) + self.gamma
165         return error

Chapter 8
Neural Networks and Deep
Learning
Warning: this is not meant to be an efficient implementation of deep learning.
If you want to do serious machine learning on medium-sized or large data,
we recommend Keras (https://keras.io) [Chollet, 2021] or PyTorch (https:
//pytorch.org), which are very efficient, particularly on GPUs. They are, how-
ever, black boxes. The AIPython neural network code should be seen like a car
engine made of glass; you can see exactly how it works, even if it is not fast.
We have followed the naming conventions of Keras for the parameters: any
parameters that are the same as in Keras have the same names.
8.1  Layers
A neural network is built from layers. In AIPython (unlike Keras and PyTorch),
activation  functions  are  treated  as  separate  layers,  which  makes  them  more
modular and the code more readable.
This provides a modular implementation of layers.   Layers can easily be
stacked in many configurations. A layer needs to implement a method to com-
pute the output values from the inputs, a method to back-propagate the error,
and a method update its parameters (if it has any) for a batch.
learnNN.py — Neural Network Learning

Data_from_files, Loss

16  class Layer(Displayable):
17      def __init__(self, nn, num_inputs=None, num_outputs=None):
18         """Abstract layer class, must be overridden.
19         nn is the neural network this layer is part of
20         num_outputs is the number of outputs for this layer.
21         """
22         self.nn = nn
23         self.num_inputs = nn.num_outputs if num_inputs is None else
num_inputs # nn output is layer's input
24         if num_outputs:
25             self.num_outputs = num_outputs
26         else:
27             self.num_outputs = self.num_inputs # same as the inputs
28         self.outputs= [0]*self.num_outputs
29         self.input_errors = [0]*self.num_inputs
30         self.weights = []
31
32      def output_values(self, input_values, training=False):
33         """Return the outputs for this layer for the given input values.
34         input_values is a list (of length self.num_inputs) of the inputs
35         returns a list of length self.num_outputs.
36         It can act differently when training and when predicting.
37         """
38         raise NotImplementedError("output_values") # abstract method
39
40      def backprop(self, out_errors):
41         """Backpropagate the errors on the outputs
42         errors is a list of output errors (of length self.num_outputs).
43         Returns list of input errors (of length self.num_inputs).
44
45         This is only called after corresponding output_values(),
46            which should remember relevant information
47         """
48         raise NotImplementedError("backprop") # abstract method
49
50  class Optimizer(Displayable):
51      def update(self, layer):
52         """updates parameters after a batch.
53         """
54         pass
8.1.1  Linear Layer
A linear layer maintains an array of weights. self.weights[i][o] is the weight
between input i and output o.  The bias is treated implicitly as the last input,
so the weight of the bias for output o is self.weights[self.num_inputs][o].
The default initialization is the Glorot uniform initializer [Glorot and Ben-
gio, 2010], which is the default in Keras.  An alternative is to provide a limit,
in which case the values are selected uniformly in the range [−limit, limit].  As
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

8.1.  Layers223
in Keras, AIpython treats initializes the bias of hidden layers to zero. The out-
put layer is treated separately, with the weights all zero except for the bias for
categorical outputs (see following exercise).
learnNN.py — (continued)
56  class Linear_complete_layer(Layer):
57      """a completely connected layer"""
58      def __init__(self, nn, num_outputs, limit=None, final_layer=False,
num_inputs=None):
59         """A completely connected linear layer.
60         nn is a neural network that the inputs come from
61         num_outputs is the number of outputs
62         the random initialization of parameters is in range [-limit,limit]
63         """
64         Layer.__init__(self, nn, num_inputs=num_inputs,
num_outputs=num_outputs)
65         if limit is None:
66             limit =math.sqrt(6/(self.num_inputs+self.num_outputs))
67         # self.weights[i][o] is the weight between input i and output o
68         if final_layer:
69              self.weights = [[0 if i < self.num_inputs
70                                  or (nn.output_type != "categorical")
71                              else 1
72                           for o in range(self.num_outputs)]
73                        for i in range(self.num_inputs+1)]
74         else:
75             self.weights = [[random.uniform(-limit, limit)
76                           if i < self.num_inputs else 0
77                           for o in range(self.num_outputs)]
78                        for i in range(self.num_inputs+1)]
79         # self.weights[i][o] is the accumulated change for a batch.
80         self.delta = [[0 for o in range(self.num_outputs)]
81                        for i in range(self.num_inputs+1)]
82
83      def output_values(self, inputs, training=False):
84         """Returns the outputs for the input values.
85         It remembers the values for the backprop.
86         """
87         self.display(3,f"Linear layer inputs: {inputs}")
88         self.inputs = inputs
89         for out in range(self.num_outputs):
90             self.outputs[out] = (sum(self.weights[inp][out]*self.inputs[inp]
91                                     for inp in range(self.num_inputs))
92                             + self.weights[self.num_inputs][out])
93         self.display(3,f"Linear layer inputs: {inputs}")
94         return self.outputs
95
96      def backprop(self, errors):
97         """Backpropagate errors, update weights, return input error.
98         errors is a list of size self.num_outputs
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

2248.  Neural Networks and Deep Learning
99         Returns errors for layer's inputs of size
100         """
101         self.display(3,f"Linear Backprop. input: {self.inputs} output
errors: {errors}")
102         for out in range(self.num_outputs):
103             for inp in range(self.num_inputs):
104                self.input_errors[inp] = self.weights[inp][out] * errors[out]
105                self.delta[inp][out] += self.inputs[inp] * errors[out]
106             self.delta[self.num_inputs][out] += errors[out]
107         self.display(3,f"Linear layer backprop input errors:
{self.input_errors}")
108         return self.input_errors

110  class ReLU_layer(Layer):
111      """Rectified linear unit (ReLU) f(z) = max(0, z).
112      The number of outputs is equal to the number of inputs.
113      """
114      def __init__(self, nn):
115         Layer.__init__(self, nn)
116
117
118      def output_values(self, input_values, training=False):
119         """Returns the outputs for the input values.
120         It remembers the input values for the backprop.
121         """
122         self.input_values = input_values
123         for i in range(self.num_inputs):
124             self.outputs[i] = max(0,input_values[i])
125         return self.outputs
126
127      def backprop(self,out_errors):
128         """Returns the derivative of the errors"""
129         for i in range(self.num_inputs):
130             self.input_errors[i] = out_errors[i] if self.input_values[i]>0
else 0
131         return self.input_errors

8.1.3  Sigmoid Layer
One of the old standards for the activation function for hidden layers is the
sigmoid. It is also used in LSTMs. It is included here to experiment with.
learnNN.py — (continued)
133  class Sigmoid_layer(Layer):
134      """sigmoids of the inputs.
135      The number of outputs is equal to the number of inputs.
136      Each output is the sigmoid of its corresponding input.
137      """
138      def __init__(self, nn):
139         Layer.__init__(self, nn)
140
141      def output_values(self, input_values, training=False):
142         """Returns the outputs for the input values.
143         It remembers the output values for the backprop.
144         """
145         for i in range(self.num_inputs):
146             self.outputs[i] = sigmoid(out_errors[i])
147         return self.outputs
148
149      def backprop(self,errors):
150         """Returns the derivative of the errors"""
151         for i in range(self.num_inputs):
152             self.input_errors[i] =
input_values[i]*out_errors[i]*(1-out_errors[i])
153         return self.input_errors

155  class NN(Learner):
156      def __init__(self, dataset, batch_gen=None, optimizer=None,
**hyperparms):
157         """Creates a neural network for a dataset
158         batch_gen is the algorithm used to generate batches (e.g., random,
streaming)
159         optimizer is the optimizer: default is SGD
160         hyperparms is the dictionary of hyperparameters for the optimizer
161         """
162         self.batch_gen = Batch_generator(dataset.train) if batch_gen is
None else batch_gen
163         self.dataset = dataset
164         self.optimizer = optimizer if optimizer else SGD
165         self.hyperparms = hyperparms
166         self.output_type = dataset.target.ftype
167         self.input_features = dataset.input_features
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

2268.  Neural Networks and Deep Learning
168         self.num_outputs = len(self.input_features) # empty NN
169         self.layers = []
170         self.bn = 0 # number of batches run
171         self.printed_heading = False # for tracing, so header printed once
172
173      def add_layer(self, layer):
174         """add a layer to the network.
175         Each layer gets number of inputs from the previous layers outputs.
176         """
177         self.layers.append(layer)
178         #if hasattr(layer,'weights'):
179         layer.optimizer = self.optimizer(layer, **self.hyperparms)
180         self.num_outputs = layer.num_outputs
181
182      def predictor(self,ex):
183         """Predicts the value of the first output for example ex.
184         """
185         values = [f(ex) for f in self.input_features]
186         for layer in self.layers:
187             values = layer.output_values(values)
188         return sigmoid(values[0]) if self.output_type =="boolean" \
189                else softmax(values, self.dataset.target.frange) if
self.output_type == "categorical" \
190                else values[0]
The learn method learns the parameters of a network.  This is like the learn()
method of linear regression (Section 7.7) except that there can be multiple out-
puts and there can be multiple optimizers.

192      def learn(self, batch_size=32, num_iter = 100, report_each=10):

criterion))
235                           for data in [self.dataset.train,
self.dataset.valid]
236                           for criterion in [Loss.accuracy, Loss.log]),
sep="")
8.3  Optimizers
The  optimizers  update  the  weights  of  a  layer  after  a  batch;  they  implement
update. The layer must have saved the weights. In layers without weights, the
weights list is empty, and update does nothing.  The backprop method stores
in layer.delta the gradient for the most recent batch. An optimizer must zero
layer.delta so the new batch can start anew.
8.3.1  Stochastic Gradient Descent
Stochastic Gradient Descent (SGD) is the most basic.  It has one hyperparame-
ter, the learning rate lr.
learnNN.py — (continued)
238  class Batch_generator(Displayable):
239      """Generator of batches.
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

2288.  Neural Networks and Deep Learning
240      Default implementation is to take a random subset of the dataset.
241      This may not be applicable for streaming data.
242      """
243      def __init__(self, dataset):
244         self.dataset = dataset
245
246      def get_batch(self, batch_size):
247         return random.sample(self.dataset, batch_size)
248
249  class SGD(Optimizer):
250      """Vanilla SGD"""
251      def __init__(self, layer, lr=0.01):
252         """layer is a layer, which contains weight and gradient matrices
253         Layers without weights have weights=[]
254         """
255         self.lr = lr
256
257      def update(self, layer):
258         """update weights of layer after a batch.
259         """
260         for inp in range(len(layer.weights)):
261             for out in range(len(layer.weights[0])):
262                layer.weights[inp][out] -= self.lr*layer.delta[inp][out]
263                layer.delta[inp][out] = 0
8.3.2  Momentum
learnNN.py — (continued)
265  class Momentum(Optimizer):
266      """SGD with momentum"""
267
268      """a completely connected layer"""
269      def __init__(self, layer, lr=0.01, momentum=0.9):
270         """
271         lr is the learning rate
272         momentum is the momentum parameter
273
274         """
275         self.lr = lr
276         self.momentum = momentum
277         layer.velocity = [[0 for _ in range((len(layer.weights[0])))]
278                            for _ in range(len(layer.weights))]
279
280
281      def update(self, layer):
282         """updates parameters after a batch with momentum"""
283         for inp in range(len(layer.weights)):
284             for out in range(len(layer.weights[0])):
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

8.3.  Optimizers229
285                layer.velocity[inp][out] =
self.momentum*layer.velocity[inp][out] -
self.lr*layer.delta[inp][out]
286                layer.weights[inp][out] += layer.velocity[inp][out]
287                layer.delta[inp][out] = 0
8.3.3  RMS-Prop
learnNN.py — (continued)
289  class RMS_Prop(Optimizer):
290      """a completely connected layer"""
291      def __init__(self, layer, rho=0.9, epsilon=1e-07, lr=0.01):
292         """A completely connected linear layer.
293         nn is a neural network that the inputs come from
294         num_outputs is the number of outputs
295         max_init is the maximum value for random initialization of
parameters
296         """
297         # layer.ms[i][o] is running average of squared gradient input i and
output o
298         layer.ms = [[0 for _ in range(len(layer.weights[0]))]
299                        for _ in range(len(layer.weights))]
300         self.rho = rho
301         self.epsilon = epsilon
302         self.lr = lr
303
304      def update(self, layer):
305         """updates parameters after a batch"""
306         for inp in range(len(layer.weights)):
307             for out in range(len(layer.weights[0])):
308                layer.ms[inp][out] = self.rho*layer.ms[inp][out]+
(1-self.rho) * layer.delta[inp][out]**2
309                layer.weights[inp][out] -= self.lr * layer.delta[inp][out] /
(layer.ms[inp][out]+self.epsilon)**0.5
310                layer.delta[inp][out] = 0
Exercise 8.3  Implement Adam [see Section 8.2.3 of Poole and Mackworth, 2023].
The implementation is slightly more complex than RMS-Prop. Try it first with the
parameter settings of Keras, as reported by Poole and Mackworth [2023].  Does it
matter if epsilon is inside or outside the square root? How sensitive is the perfor-
mance to the parameter settings?
Exercise 8.4  Both Goodfellow, Bengio, and Courville [2016] and Poole and Mack-
worth  [2023]  find  the  gradient  by  dividing self.delta[inp][out]  by  the  batch
size, but some of the above code doesn’t.  To make code with dividing and with-
out dividing the same, the step sizes need to be different by a factor of the batch
size.   Find a reasonable step size using an informal hyperparameter tuning;  try
some orders of magnitude of the step size to see what works best. What happens
if the batch size is changed, but the step size is unchanged? (Try orders of magni-
tude difference is step sizes.) For each of the update method, which works better:
dividing by the step size or not?

312  from utilities import flip
313  class Dropout_layer(Layer):
314      """Dropout layer
315      """
316
317      def __init__(self, nn, rate=0):
318         """
319         rate is fraction of the input units to drop. 0 =< rate < 1
320         """
321         self.rate = rate
322         Layer.__init__(self, nn)
323         self.mask = [0]*self.num_inputs
324
325      def output_values(self, input_values, training=False):
326         """Returns the outputs for the input values.
327         It remembers the input values and mask for the backprop.
328         """
329         if training:
330             scaling = 1/(1-self.rate)
331             for i in range(self.num_inputs):
332                self.mask[i] = 0 if flip(self.rate) else 1
333                input_values[i] = self.mask[i]*input_values[i]*scaling
334         return input_values
335
336      def backprop(self, output_errors):
337         """Returns the derivative of the errors"""
338         for i in range(self.num_inputs):
339             self.input_errors[i] = output_errors[i]*self.mask[i]
340         return self.input_errors

NN_from_arch(dataset, architecture, optimizer, parameters) creates
a  generic  feedforward  neural  network  with  ReLU  activation  for  the  hidden
layers.   The dataset is needed as the input and output is determined by the
data. The architecture is a list of the sizes of hidden layers. If the architecture is
the empty list, this corresponds to linear or logistic regression.  The optimizer
is one of SGD, Momentum, RMS_Prop.

388  class NN_from_arch(NN):
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

2328.  Neural Networks and Deep Learning
389      def __init__(self, data, arch, optimizer=SGD, **hyperparms):
390         """arch is a list of widths of the hidden layers from bottom up.
391         opt is an optimizer (one of: SGD, Momentum, RMS_Prop)
392         hyperparms is the parameters of the optimizer
393         returns a neural network with ReLU activations on hidden layers
394         """
395         NN.__init__(self, data, optimizer=optimizer, **hyperparms)
396         for width in arch:
397             self.add_layer(Linear_complete_layer(self,width))
398             self.add_layer(ReLU_layer(self))
399         output_size = len(data.target.frange) if data.target.ftype ==
"categorical" else 1
400         self.add_layer(Linear_complete_layer(self,output_size,
final_layer=True))
401         hyperparms_string =','.join(f"{p}={v}" for p,v in
hyperparms.items())
402         self.name = f"NN({arch},{optimizer.__name__}({hyperparms_string}))"
403
404      def __str__(self):
405          return self.name

8.6  Plotting Performance
You can plot the performance of various algorithms on the training and vali-
dation sets.
Figure 8.1 shows the training and validation performance on the SPECT
dataset for the architectures given.  The legend give the architecture, the op-
timizer, the options, and the evaluation dataset.  The architecture [] is for lo-
gistic regression.  Notice how, as the network gets larger the better they fit the
training data, but can overfit more as the number of steps increases (probably
because the probabilities get more extreme).  These figures suggest that early
stopping after 200-300 steps might provide best test performance.
The plot_algs method does all combinations of architectures, optimizers
and learning rates.  It plots both learning and validation errors.  The output is
only readable if two of these are singletons, and one varies (as in the examples).
The plot_algs_opts method is more general as it allows for different com-
binations of architectures,  optimizers and learning rates,  which makes more
sense if, for example, the learning rate is set depending on the architecture and
optimizer. It also allows other hyperparameters to be specified and varied.

417  def plot_algs(data, archs=[[3]], opts=[SGD],lrs=[0.1, 0.01,0.001,0.0001],
418                    criterion=crit, num_steps=1000):
419      args = []

424      plot_algs_opts(data, args, criterion, num_steps)

427      """args is a list of (architecture, optimizer, parameters)
428        for each of the corresponding triples it plots the learning rate"""
429      for (arch, opt, hyperparms) in args:
430         nn = NN_from_arch(data, arch, opt, **hyperparms)

8.7  Language Tokenizer
Variants of the Byte Pair Encoding Algorithm (BPE) [Sennrich, Haddow, and
Birch, 2016; Radford, Wu, Child, Luan, Amodei, and Sutskever, 2019] are the
standard method for modern language models.  The idea is simple.  All of the
characters are tokens. A new token is created by finding the most common pair
of tokens and making that a new token.  For example, of “di” and “ffere” are
tokens, they can be joined to form the new token “differe”. A tokenizer can be
trained on a subset of the training corpus.
There are three main challenges in using this algorithm:
•  Building an efficient encoder which translates raw text (sequence of char-
acters) into a sequence of tokens.  It is important that this is efficient, as
it will be used to encode the full training corpus.  In the code here, we
stream the text (using generators) building tokens using a variant of a
trie, which branches on the next character, and returns the longest token
that fits.
•  Building the data structures needed to determine which tokens to create
next.  The problem is counting the number of occurrences of each pair
of tokens in the corpus to determine which pair occurs the most often.
Here we store a data structure that is counts the number of times a pair
of tokens appears (only representing the pairs that appear in the training
corpus).   For  each  new  token,  the  pairs  data  structure  is  rebuilt  by  re-
reading the corpus, which is not be stored, but streamed on demand. This
may be reasonable as the number of pairs is much less than the size of the
corpus, and this is only done once initially, typically on a much smaller
corpus.   [To make this more efficient,  an “obvious” way is to take the,
say, the 10 top pairs to make into tokens. Why might this give something
different?]
•  Decoding,  mapping  a  sequence  of  tokens  to  a  sequence  of  characters.
This is much simpler than the other two challenges.  In the code below,
the tokens are represented as the corresponding string. More commonly,
tokens are numbered sequentially and there needs to be a mapping from
index to token.
The corpus is taken from Project Gutenberg https://www.gutenberg.org, a
library of free, out-of-copyright books.  As our corpus, we use the text version
of a diverse set of books:
•  pg11 “Alice’s Adventures in Wonderland” by Lewis Carroll
•  pg45 “Anne of Green Gables” by Lucy Maud Montgomery
•  pg74 “The Adventures of Tom Sawyer, Complete” by Mark Twain
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

8.7.  Language Tokenizer237
•  pg84 “Frankenstein; Or, The Modern Prometheus” by Mary Wollstonecraft
Shelley
•  pg1342 “Pride and Prejudice” by Jane Austen
•  pg1661 “The Adventures of Sherlock Holmes” by Arthur Conan Doyle
With a total of 2.925 million chars for 3.044 MB, uncompressed.

The following generates the characters from the files in the corpus.  It replaces
newlines with spaces and makes multiple spaces into a single space. Note that
the standard generation (while c in file) generates lines. It is implemented as a
pipeline of two generators: file_to_chars0 replaces newlines with spaces and
file_to_chars replaces multiple spaces with a single one.
learnTokenizer.py — (continued)
26  def file_to_chars0(file_name):
27      with open(file_name,'r') as file:
28         while c := file.read(1):
29             if c == "\n":
30                yield " "
31             else:
32                yield c
33
34  def file_to_chars(file_name):
35      f2ch = file_to_chars0(file_name)
36      try:
37         lookahead = next(f2ch)
38      except StopIteration:    # for empty files
39         return
40      for ch in f2ch:
41         if lookahead !='' or ch !='': #reject spaces after space
42             yield lookahead
43             lookahead = ch
44      yield lookahead
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

The following are utilities used by the code below.  They are probably in
some libraries, but it is simple to write them.

46  def sequence_generators(*gens):
47      """
48      Given generators as arguments, generates the values of the generators
in turn
49      """
50      for g in gens:
51         # yield from g # does not work
52         for e in g:
53             yield e
54
55  class Multiset(object):
56      def __init__(self):
57         self.bag = {}
58
59      def add(self, elt):
60         if elt in self.bag:
61             self.bag[elt] += 1
62         else:
63             self.bag[elt] = 1
64
65      def items(self):
66         return self.bag.items()
The Tokenizer class builds three data structures:
•  A list tokens, so that tokens[k] is the string for token k, where a token is
an integer.
• token_trie is a dictionary that maps characters into a pair consisting of a
token (or None) and a dictionary of the same form. It is used to map text
into tokens.  If the sequence of characters that reached a pair is a token,
that token is the first element of the pair.  If the sequence of characters
that reached the pair is not a token, the first element of the pair is None.
• token_pairs is a multiset of token pairs so that token_pairs[(t1,t2)] is
the number of times token t1 is followed by token t2.
If any pair of tokens can form new tokens, it is possible to have "the", " the",
"the " (note different placement of a space) all be different tokens. Think about
how these pair up with "cat",  " cat" or "cat " to form "the cat" (perhaps with
spaces at the ends). Not allowing spaces to join to other tokens gives subword
tokens, where no tokens contain spaces.  If the second of the pair cannot be a
space, spaces are only allowed at the start or middle of tokens. We define three
different versions, which can be selected using
learnTokenizer.py — (continued)
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

8.7.  Language Tokenizer239
68  class Legal_token(object):
69      """Possible implementations of legal_token_pair in Tokenizer.
70      Must be defined before Tokenizer.
71      This is a separate class (instead of, say, making the alternatives
72      subclasses of Tokenizer) because someone might want to change the
73      criterion (e.g., starting with work and changing to space).
74      """
75      def any_pair(toks, previous, token):
76         """All pairs are legal
77          """
78         return True  # no restrictions
79
80      def word(toks, previous, token):
81         """pair is illegal if either is a space (or punctuation).
82         Creates subword tokenization
83         Warning: the punctuation characters must be in toks.token_trie
before calling.
84          """
85         space_like_tokens = {toks.token_trie[ch][0] for ch in " ,.;"}
86         return not (token in space_like_tokens
87                    or previous in space_like_tokens)
88
89      def space(toks, previous, token):
90         """The second element of the pair cannot be a space, it only allows
spaces at start and middle of tokens:
91         """
92         return ( token not in {toks.token_trie[ch][0] for ch in " ,.;"})
learnTokenizer.py — (continued)
94  class Tokenizer(Displayable):
95      def __init__(self, corpus = train_corpus,
96                       corpus_folder = train_corpus_folder,
97                       legal_token_pair = Legal_token.word,
98                    load_from_file = None):
99         self.corpus = corpus
100         self.corpus_folder = corpus_folder
101         self.legal_token_pair = legal_token_pair
102         if load_from_file is not None:
103             self.load(load_from_file)
104         else:
105             self.tokens = ["[UNK]", " ", ",", ".",";", "<start>","<end>"] #
this could also be populated with characters in order.
106             self.token_trie = {c:[t,{}] for t,c in enumerate(self.tokens)}
# char -> [tok, dict] tok is a token or None. dict is a
token_trie
107             self.token_pairs = Multiset() # multiset of pairs of tokens
108             for file_name in corpus:
109                previous, _ = self.token_trie["<start>"] # start token
110                for ch in file_to_chars(corpus_folder+file_name):
111                    if ch not in self.token_trie:
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

2408.  Neural Networks and Deep Learning
112                        token = len(self.tokens)
113                        self.tokens.append(ch)
114                        self.token_trie[ch]= [token, {}]
115                    else:
116                        token,_ = self.token_trie[ch]
117                    if legal_token_pair(self, previous, token):
118                        self.token_pairs.add((previous, token))
119                    previous = token
120                if legal_token_pair(self, previous, token):
121                    self.token_pairs.add((previous, token))
122             self.display(1, f"Corpus has {len(self.tokens)}
single-character tokens.")
123
124      def decode(self, token_list):
125         """ given a list of tokens, creates the corresponding string
126         """
127         res = "".join(self.tokens[tok] for tok in token_list)
128         return res
One complication  the code  needs to handle  is that  not all prefixes  of to-
kens are tokens.  For example, suppose "ahead" is a token (created by joining
"a" and "head"), and none of its prefixes other than "a" are tokens.  the string
"ahh..." returns the token "a", and "hh" becomes a lookahead string, which the
parser should start again with and then use the rest of the text.   The looka-
head  might  not  be  used  up  by  when  generating  the  subsequent  token.   For
example, if "ahead" were a token, the text "ahearne..." would have "ahear" read
before it can conclude it is not the token "ahead", which may become the tokens
"a"+"he"+"ar".
The following tokenizes a corpus (corpus_to_tokens) or any string (tokenize).
These generate tokens.
learnTokenizer.py — (continued)
130      def corpus_to_tokens(self):
131         for file_name in self.corpus:
132             char_generator = file_to_chars(self.corpus_folder+file_name)
133             yield from self.tokenize(char_generator)
134
135      def tokenize(self, string_input):
136         """generate tokens from a string.
137         """
138         gen_input = (c for c in string_input)
139         lookahead = []
140         unused = []
141         ended = False
142         while not ended:
143             dic = self.token_trie
144             unusedlist = list(unused)
145             self.display(3,f"start while {lookahead=} {unusedlist=}")
146             unused = (e for e in lookahead + unusedlist)
147             ended = True
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

8.7.  Language Tokenizer241
148             for next_char in sequence_generators(unused, gen_input):
149                if next_char not in self.token_trie:
150                    next_char = self.tokens[0] # unknown character
151                ended = False
152                self.display(3,f"{next_char=}")
153                if next_char in dic:
154                    tok,dic = dic[next_char]
155                    if tok is not None: #dictionary entry is a token
156                        self.display(3,f"token={tok=} {self.tokens[tok]}")
157                        token=tok
158                        lookahead=[]
159                    else:
160                        lookahead.append(next_char)
161                        self.display(3,f"tok=None {token=}
{self.tokens[token]} {lookahead=}")
162                else:
163                    lookahead.append(next_char)
164                    self.display(3,f"in else yielding {token=}
{self.tokens[token]} {lookahead=}")
165                    yield token
166                    break
167             else:
168                if not ended:
169                   self.display(3,f"for ended yielding {token=}
{self.tokens[token]} {lookahead=} {ended=}")
170                   yield token
Exercise 8.7   Consider what happens when a token could be created in two dif-
ferent ways, such as “i”+“ng” or “in”+“g”. Before the token is created how would
“ing” be tokenized? Would it be tokenized twice? Why or why not?
Given the number of tokens to end up with, create_tokens creates tokens
from the current corpus.  It assumes that there is a current multiset of pairs of
tokens, from which it chooses the most common pair. It adds this to the token
trie.  The new token can be a subword of a current token or could extend the
trie.  It uses the generated sequence of tokens generated by the current tokens
to create a new the multiset of pairs.
learnTokenizer.py — (continued)
172      def create_tokens(self, num_tokens):
173         while len(self.tokens) < num_tokens:
174             count, (t0,t1) = max((c,p) for (p,c) in
self.token_pairs.items())
175             new_token = len(self.tokens)
176             token_string = self.tokens[t0]+self.tokens[t1] # append tokens
177             self.display(0, f'{len(self.tokens)}: Creating token:
{self.tokens[t0]!r}+{self.tokens[t1]!r}'
178                           f' -> {token_string!r} {count=}')
179             self.tokens.append(token_string)
180             self.add_to_trie(token_string, new_token)
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

2428.  Neural Networks and Deep Learning
181
182             # Rebuild pairs from scratch
183             self.token_pairs = Multiset()
184             generate_tokens = self.corpus_to_tokens()
185             prev = next(generate_tokens)
186             for token in generate_tokens:
187                if self.legal_token_pair(self, prev, token):
188                    self.token_pairs.add((prev, token))
189                prev = token
190
191      def add_to_trie(self, token_string, new_token):
192         """Adds a new token to the current trie
193         token_string is the string of the token
194         new_token is the index into tokens list.
195         """
196         token_trie = self.token_trie
197         for ch in token_string[:-1]:
198             if ch in token_trie:
199                (_, token_trie) = token_trie[ch]
200             else:
201                dct = {}
202                token_trie[ch] = [None,dct]
203                token_trie = dct
204         if token_string[-1] in token_trie:
205             token_trie[token_string[-1]][0] = new_token
206         else:
207             token_trie[token_string[-1]] = [new_token,{}]
208
209      # Saving and loading computed data structures
210      def save(self, filename="tokens.pkl"):
211         file = open(filename,'wb')
212         pickle.dump((self.tokens,self.token_trie,self.token_pairs), file)
213      def load(self, filename="tokens.pkl"):
214         file = open(filename,'rb')
215         (self.tokens,self.token_trie,self.token_pairs) = pickle.load(file)
216         self.display(0, f"Corpus has {len(self.tokens)} tokens.")

8.8  Bigram with Empirical Probabilities
Except for the restrictions on what can be in a token, the multiset token_pairs
contains enough information to determine the probability distribution of the
words given the previous word, which is a bigram model (an n-gram model
where n = 2).  The model below uses empirical probabilities without regular-
ization.  (Regularization could be to use pseudo-counts as in a Dirichlet dis-
tribution.)  Empirical probabilities may be for bigrams when generating text,
but not for recognition as they give zero probability for pairs not in the train-
ing set.  For n-grams for larger n, empirical probabilities can be undefined for
word sequences not in the training set, and so are not suitable for generation
or recognition.
learnNgram.py — Bigram and N-gram with embeddings

15  class Bigram(Displayable):
16      def __init__(self, tokenizer):
17         self.tokenizer = tokenizer
18         self.ttc = [[0 for _ in tokenizer.tokens] for _ in
tokenizer.tokens] # dense
19         self.counts=[0 for _ in tokenizer.tokens]
20         # ttc[token1][token2] is the number of times token 1 is followed by
token 2
21         generate_tokens = tokenizer.corpus_to_tokens()
22         prev = next(generate_tokens)
23         self.counts[prev] = 1
24         for token in generate_tokens:
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

2448.  Neural Networks and Deep Learning
25             self.ttc[prev][token] += 1
26             self.counts[token] += 1
27             prev = token
28
29      def generate(self, prompt, length, temp=1):
30         res = prompt+"" # copy prompt
31         ti = list(self.tokenizer.tokenize(prompt))[-1] # last token
32         for i in range(length):
33             ti = sample_i(self.ttc[ti], temp=temp)
34             res += self.tokenizer.tokens[ti]
35         return res
36
37      def dist(self, prompt):
38         """returns a sorted list of prob,string
39         """
40         ti = list(self.tokenizer.tokenize(prompt))[-1] # last token
41         unnorm = self.ttc[ti]
42         tot = sum(unnorm)
43         return sorted([(n/tot,self.tokenizer.tokens[i]) for (i,n) in
enumerate(unnorm) if n>0])
44
45
46  def sample_i(probs, temp=1):
47      """Sample from an unnormalized probability distribution.
48      probs is {val:p} dictionary or a list of nonnegative numbers
49      """
50      #keys = range(len(probs)) if isinstance(probs,list) else probs.keys()
51      if not isinstance(probs,dict):
52         probs = {i:v for (i,v) in enumerate(list(probs))}
53      if temp != 1:
54        probs = {k:p**(1/temp) for k,p in probs.items()}
55      tot = sum(probs.values())
56      sm = 0
57      p = random.random()*tot
58      for i in probs:
59        sm += probs[i]
60        if p <= sm:
61            return i
62      assert False, f"sample_i has error with {probs=}"
63

#"tokens.pkl")

8.9  N-Grams with token embeddings
Going beyond bigrams to arbitrary n-grams (looking n− 1 tokens in the past)
using empirical probabilities is problematic as for many sequences of words
there is no data. An alternative to using empirical probabilities is to use token
features, represented as vectors.
The one-hot layer is an instance of the linear complete layer, designed when
one of the inputs is 1 and others are zero, and the inputs are given (not learned)
There are two main changes:
•  It does not need to multiply by 0 or 1, but selects the input that would be
multiplied by 1.
•  Backprop does not need to return an error as the inputs are always ob-
served.
learnNgram.py — (continued)
77  from learnNN import NN, Layer, Linear_complete_layer, Batch_generator,
RMS_Prop

80  class One_Hot_Layer(Linear_complete_layer):
81      """A complete linear layer where one input is 1 and the others are 0.
82      No need to multiple by 1 or 0.
83       """
84      def __init__(self, nn, num_inputs, num_outputs, limit=None,
final_layer=False):
85           Linear_complete_layer.__init__(self, nn, num_outputs, limit,
final_layer,
86                                           num_inputs=num_inputs)
87
88      def output_values(self, input, training=False):
89         """Returns the outputs for the input values.
90         It remembers the input for the backprop.
91         """
92         self.display(3,f"One-hot layer inputs: {input}")
93         self.input=input
94         for out in range(self.num_outputs):
95             self.outputs[out] = (self.weights[input][out]
96                               + self.weights[self.num_inputs][out])
97         self.display(3,f"One-hot layer {input=}, {self.outputs=}")
98         return self.outputs
99
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

2468.  Neural Networks and Deep Learning
100      def backprop(self, errors):
101         """Backpropagate errors, update weights, return input error.
102         errors is a list of size self.num_outputs
103         No need to return errors for layer's input as input is always data
104         """
105         self.display(3,f"One-hot Backprop. input: {self.input} output
errors: {errors}")
106         for out in range(self.num_outputs):
107             inp=self.input
108             #self.input_errors[inp] = self.weights[inp][out] * errors[out]
109             self.delta[inp][out] += self.input * errors[out]
110             self.delta[self.num_inputs][out] += errors[out]
111         #self.display(3,f"One-hot layer backprop input errors:
{self.input_errors}")
112         #return self.input_errors
113
114  class Concat_Layers(Layer):
115      """A layer created by concatenating a list of layers.
116      Each layer has a single input value;
117           the input of the concatenation is list of these values
118      Output of the concatenation is the concatenation of the outputs
119      """
120      def __init__(self, nn, layers):
121         num_outputs = sum(lay.num_outputs for lay in layers)
122         self.display(3, f"Concat_Layers {layers=} {num_outputs=}")
123         Layer.__init__(self, nn, num_outputs)
124         self.layers = layers
125
126      def output_values(self, input_values, training=False):
127         ov = []
128         for lay,inp in zip(self.layers, input_values):
129             ov += lay.output_values(inp)
130         return ov
131
132      def backprop(self, out_errors):
133         # need to unpack the array of outputs to the output of each layer
134         res=[]
135         for lay in self.layers:
136            res.append(lay.backprop(out_errors[:lay.num_outputs]))
137            out_errors = out_errors[lay.num_outputs:]
138         return res
139

153  class NGram_Batch_Generator(Batch_generator):
154      def __init__(self, tokenizer, n):
155         self.tokenizer = tokenizer
156         self.n = n
157         self.ngrams = self.Ngram_generator() # data generator
158
159      def Ngram_generator(self):
160         while True: # keep going through the corpus
161             token_gen = self.tokenizer.corpus_to_tokens()
162             self.context = [next(token_gen) for i in range(self.n)]
163             for token in token_gen:
164                yield self.context
165                self.context = self.context[1:]+[token]
166
167      def get_batch(self, batch_size):
168         return [next(self.ngrams) for i in range(batch_size)]
169

174  class N_gram(NN):
175      def __init__(self, n, tokenizer, hidden_size, optimizer=None,
**hyperparms):
176         self.n = n
177         self.tokenizer = tokenizer
178         dataset = N_gram_dataset(n, tokenizer)
179         NN.__init__(self, dataset,
batch_gen=NGram_Batch_Generator(tokenizer, n),
180                         optimizer=optimizer, **hyperparms)
181         self.add_layer(
182             Concat_Layers(self,
183                    [One_Hot_Layer(self,
len(dataset.tokenizer.tokens),hidden_size) for i in
range(n-1)]))
184         # add hidden layers here
185         self.add_layer(Linear_complete_layer(self, len(tokenizer.tokens)))
186

The above code is very slow.  The neural network code is interpreted and
run sequentially.  It was not written to be efficient.  For example, it appends
the bias to the output of a layer to gets the next input. Modern neural network
toolkits are orders of magnitude faster.  They compile to a form so that at run
time the code is only doing the appropriate arithmetic functions, such as addi-
tion and multiplication, and is run on parallel hardware that is specialized to
these forms expressions.
Kereas  is  a  front  end  for  many  efficient  back  ends.   It  is  available  from
Python  distributions  such  as https://anaconda.org/channels/conda-forge/
packages/keras/overview.  See https://keras.io/getting_started/.  Chollet
and Watson [2025] provides a good introduction to Keras.
For the following code to work, you need to download the test files, and
create and save a tokenization.

The following implements an embedding based N-gram model.   Each word
has an embedding.  The embedding for the context_size previous words are
concatenated, and a fully connected layer predicts the next word.

79  class NGram(Displayable):
80      def __init__(self,
81                 context_size = context_size,
82                 embedding_size = embedding_size,
83                 dataset = None,
84                 tokenizer = tok
85                 ):
86
87         self.context_size = context_size
88         self.embedding_size = embedding_size
89         if dataset is None:

91                data = corpus_tokens[:-self.context_size],
92                targets = corpus_tokens[self.context_size:],
93                sequence_length = self.context_size,
94                shuffle=True
95                )
96         else:
97             self.dataset = dataset
98         self.tokenizer = tokenizer
99         inputs = keras.Input(shape=(self.context_size,), dtype="int32")
100         embedding = keras.layers.Embedding(
101             input_dim = len(self.tokenizer.tokens),
102             output_dim = self.embedding_size
103             #,mask_zero=True
104             )
105         emb = embedding(inputs)
106         con = keras.layers.Flatten()(emb)
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

8.9.  N-Grams with token embeddings251
107         output = keras.layers.Dense(len(self.tokenizer.tokens),
activation="softmax")(con)
108         self.model = keras.Model(inputs, output,
name=f"NGram_{context_size}_{embedding_size}")
109
110         self.model.compile(optimizer=optimizer,
111               loss="sparse_categorical_crossentropy",
112               metrics=["sparse_categorical_accuracy"])
113         print(f"NGram({context_size=}, {embedding_size=})")
114         self.model.summary()   # get statistics
115
116      def generate(self, prompt, max_length=150, temp=1):
117         """generates a completion adding max_length tokens.
118         this is a greedy genertion; picking the most likely at each step
119         filler is used so a prompt less than the context_size does not give
an error
120         """
121         filler = [random.randint(0,len(self.tokenizer.tokens)-1) for _ in
range(self.context_size)]
122         tokens = filler + list(self.tokenizer.tokenize(prompt))
123         self.display(2, f"{tokens=}")
124         prompt_length = len(tokens)
125         for _ in range(max_length):
126             prediction =
self.model(keras.ops.convert_to_numpy([tokens[-self.context_size:]]))
127             pred_list = prediction.numpy().flatten()
128             top5 = sorted([(p,self.tokenizer.tokens[i]) for (i,p) in
enumerate(pred_list)], reverse=True)[:5]
129             self.display(1,[self.tokenizer.tokens[i] for i in
tokens[-self.context_size:]],top5)
130             next_val = sample_i(pred_list, temp=temp)
#np.argmax(prediction).item()
131             self.display(1,f" -> {self.tokenizer.tokens[next_val]}")
132             tokens.append(next_val)
133         #print(f"{tokens=}")
134         return "".join(self.tokenizer.tokens[t] for t in
tokens[self.context_size:])
135
136      def save(self, filename="ngram"):
137         """save model parameters"""
138         self.model.save(f"{filename}_{self.context_size}_{self.embedding_size}.keras")
139      def load(self, filename="ngram.pkl"):
140         self.model =
keras.models.load_model(f"{filename}_{self.context_size}_{self.embedding_size}.keras")
141         self.model.summary()   # get statistics

Chapter 9
Reasoning with Uncertainty
9.1  Representing Probabilistic Models
A probabilistic model uses the same definition of a variable as a CSP (Section
4.1.1, page 69).  A variable consists of a name, a domain and an optional (x,y)
position (for displaying).  The domain of a variable is a list or a tuple, as the
ordering matters for some representation of factors.
9.2  Representing Factors
A factor is, mathematically, a function from variables into a number;  that is,
given a value for each of its variable, it gives a number.  Factors are used for
conditional probabilities,  utilities in the next chapter,  and are explicitly con-
structed by some algorithms (in particular, variable elimination).
A variable assignment, or just an assignment, is represented as a{variable :
value} dictionary.  A factor can be evaluated when all of its variables are as-
signed.  This is implemented in the can_evaluate method which can be over-
ridden for representations that don’t require all variable be assigned (such as
decision trees).  The method get_value evaluates the factor for an assignment.
The assignment can include extra variables not in the factor. This method needs
to be defined for every subclass.
probFactors.py — Factors for graphical models
11  from display import Displayable
12  import math
13
14  class Factor(Displayable):
15      nextid=0 # each factor has a unique identifier; for printing
16

to variables.
33         Needs to be defined for each subclass.
34         """

36         raise NotImplementedError("get_value") # abstract method
The method __str__ returns a brief definition (like “f7(X,Y,Z)”).The method

ments of values to variables, and the corresponding value.

42      def to_table(self, variables=None, given={}):

44         Allows for an arbitrary variable ordering.
45         variables is a list of the variables in the factor
46         (can contain other variables)"""

50             variables = [v for v in variables if v in self.variables and v
not in given]

52         return head+"\n"+self.ass_to_str(variables, given, variables)

9.3  Conditional Probability Distributions
A conditional probability distribution (CPD) is a factor that represents a con-
ditional  probability.   A  CPD  representing  P(X |  Y
1
. . . Y
k
)  is  a  factor,  which
given values for X and each Y
i
returns a number.
probFactors.py — (continued)
67  class CPD(Factor):
68      def __init__(self, child, parents):
69         """represents P(variable | parents)
70         """
71         self.parents = parents
72         self.child = child
73         Factor.__init__(self, parents+[child], name=f"Probability")
74
75      def __str__(self):
76         """A brief description of a factor using in tracing"""
77         if self.parents:
78             return f"P({self.child}|{','.join(str(p) for p in
self.parents)})"
79         else:
80             return f"P({self.child})"
81
82      __repr__ = __str__
A constant CPD has no parents, and has probability 1 when the variable has
the value specified, and 0 when the variable has a different value.
probFactors.py — (continued)
84  class ConstantCPD(CPD):
85      def __init__(self, variable, value):
86         CPD.__init__(self, variable, [])
87         self.value = value
88      def get_value(self, assignment):
89         return 1 if self.value==assignment[self.child] else 0

9.3.1  Logistic Regression
A logistic regression CPD, for Boolean variable X represents P(X=True| Y
1
. . . Y
k
),
using k + 1 real-valued weights so
P(X=True| Y
1
. . . Y
k
) = sigmoid(w
0
+
∑
i
w
i
Y
i
)
where for Boolean Y
i
, True is represented as 1 and False as 0.
probFactors.py — (continued)
91  from learnLinear import sigmoid, logit
92
93  class LogisticRegression(CPD):
94      def __init__(self, child, parents, weights):
95         """A logistic regression representation of a conditional
probability.
96         child is the Boolean (or 0/1) variable whose CPD is being defined
97         parents is the list of parents
98         weights is list of parameters, such that weights[i+1] is the weight
for parents[i]
99           weights[0] is the bias.
100         """
101         assert len(weights) == 1+len(parents)
102         CPD.__init__(self, child, parents)
103         self.weights = weights
104
105      def get_value(self,assignment):
106         assert self.can_evaluate(assignment)
107         prob = sigmoid(self.weights[0]
108                        + sum(self.weights[i+1]*assignment[self.parents[i]]
109                                 for i in range(len(self.parents))))
110         if assignment[self.child]: #child is true
111             return prob
112         else:
113             return (1-prob)
9.3.2  Noisy-or
A noisy-or, for Boolean variable X with Boolean parents Y
1
. . . Y
k
is parametrized
by k + 1 parameters p
0
, p
1
, . . . , p
k
, where each 0 ≤ p
i
≤ 1.  The semantics is de-
fined as though there are k + 1 hidden variables Z
0
, Z
1
. . . Z
k
, where P(Z
0
) = p
0
and P(Z
i
| Y
i
) = p
i
for i≥ 1, and where X is true if and only if Z
0
∨ Z
1
∨···∨ Z
k
(where∨ is “or”).  Thus X is false if all of the Z
i
are false.  Intuitively, Z
0
is the
probability of X when all Y
i
are false and each Z
i
is a noisy (probabilistic) mea-
sure that Y
i
makes X true, and X only needs one to make it true.

9.3.3  Tabular Factors and Prob
A tabular factor is a factor that represents each assignment of values to vari-
ables separately.  It is represented by a Python array (or Python dict).  If the
variables are V
1
, V
2
, . . . , V
k
, the value of f(V
1
=  v
1
, V
2
=  v
1
, . . . , V
k
=  v
k
) is
stored in f[v
1
][v
2
] . . . [v
k
].
If the domain of V
i
is [0, . . . , n
i
− 1] it can be represented as an array.  Oth-
erwise it can use a dictionary. Python is nice in that it doesn’t care, whether an
array or dict is used except when enumerating the values; enumerating a dict
gives the keys (the variables) but enumerating an array gives the values. So we
had to be careful not to enumerate the values.
probFactors.py — (continued)
136  class TabFactor(Factor):
137
138      def __init__(self, variables, values, name=None):
139         Factor.__init__(self, variables, name=name)
140         self.values = values
141
142      def get_value(self, assignment):
143         return self.get_val_rec(self.values, self.variables, assignment)
144
145      def get_val_rec(self, value, variables, assignment):
146         if variables == []:
147            return value
148         else:
149             return self.get_val_rec(value[assignment[variables[0]]],
150                                      variables[1:],assignment)

155         """Creates a factor from a conditional probability table, cpt

159         self.child = var
160         self.parents = pars
9.3.4  Decision Tree Representations of Factors
A decision tree representation of a conditional probability of a child variable is
either:
• IFeq(var, val, true_cond, false_cond) where true_cond and false_cond
are decision trees. true_cond is used if variable var has value val in an
assignment; false_cond is used if var has a different value
•  a deterministic functions that has probability 1 if a parent has the same
value as the child (using SameAs(parent))
•  a distribution over the child variable (using Dist(dict)).
Note that not all parents need to be assigned to evaluate the decision tree; it
only needs a branch down the tree that gives the distribution.
probFactors.py — (continued)
162  class ProbDT(CPD):
163      def __init__(self, child, parents, dt):
164         CPD.__init__(self, child, parents)
165         self.dt = dt
166
167      def get_value(self, assignment):
168         return self.dt.get_value(assignment, self.child)
169
170      def can_evaluate(self, assignment):
171         return self.child in assignment and self.dt.can_evaluate(assignment)
Decision trees are made up of conditions; here equality of a value and a vari-
able:
probFactors.py — (continued)
173  class IFeq:
174      def __init__(self, var, val, true_cond, false_cond):
175         self.var = var
https://aipython.orgVersion 0.9.18September 18, 2026

9.3.  Conditional Probability Distributions261
176         self.val = val
177         self.true_cond = true_cond
178         self.false_cond = false_cond
179
180      def get_value(self, assignment, child):
181         """  IFeq(var, val, true_cond, false_cond)
182         value of true_cond is used if var has value val in assignment,
183         value of false_cond is used if var has a different value
184         """
185         if assignment[self.var] == self.val:
186             return self.true_cond.get_value(assignment, child)
187         else:
188             return self.false_cond.get_value(assignment,child)
189
190      def can_evaluate(self, assignment):
191         if self.var not in assignment:
192             return False
193         elif assignment[self.var] == self.val:
194             return self.true_cond.can_evaluate(assignment)
195         else:
196             return self.false_cond.can_evaluate(assignment)
The following is a deterministic function that is true if the parent has the
same value as the child. This is used for deterministic conditional probabilities
(as is common for causal models, as described in Chapter 11).
probFactors.py — (continued)
198  class SameAs:
199      def __init__(self, parent):
200         """1 when child has same value as parent, otherwise 0"""
201         self.parent = parent
202
203      def get_value(self, assignment, child):
204         return 1 if assignment[child]==assignment[self.parent] else 0
205
206      def can_evaluate(self, assignment):
207         return self.parent in assignment
At the leaves are distributions over the child variable.
probFactors.py — (continued)
209  class Dist:
210      def __init__(self, dist):
211         """Dist is an array or dictionary indexed by value of current
child"""
212         self.dist = dist
213
214      def get_value(self, assignment, child):
215         return self.dist[assignment[child]]
216
217      def can_evaluate(self, assignment):
218         return True

16  class GraphicalModel(Displayable):
17      """The class of graphical models.
18      A graphical model consists of a title, a set of variables and a set of

19
